Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I think they’re telling me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear a voice speaking when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… in the afternoon mostly. Sounds clear… like he’s right there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear that voice, does it ever tell you to do things?\n\nPatient: No… not telling me to do things. Mostly it just calls my name, or hums a church song… like he’s out there on the porch.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences started, did you have a stroke, head injury, seizure, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not that I can remember. Nothing like that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Maybe… about nine months, I’d say. Not every day. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. I still love my family, I do. But a lot of days I just sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden… choir practice… I just let it pass. Don’t feel much like getting up for it.\n\nDoctor: Did these experiences start suddenly, over just a few days?\n\nPatient: No… not over just a few days. It’s been more… gradual, I guess. Since after my close friend passed away. About a year and five months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not most of the day, I don’t think. I get low sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m sure about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your emotions feel blunted or that it’s hard to show your feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe. I still care about my family, but I don’t show much, I guess. Most days I just sit there at the kitchen table and don’t say a whole lot. The garden, choir… I just let it go by. Don’t feel like moving much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with your memory?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… maybe little things now and then, but I haven’t really noticed anything big with my memory.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor told you that you have Parkinson’s disease?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anyone’s told me that.\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medicines in the months before these experiences began?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I can’t think of any new pills around then. when the house is quiet, I hear my old friend call my name from the porch or hum a choir song, clear like he is standing right there.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At domino games I have started tapping the tiles in the same pattern over and over and holding my hand up like I am stopping traffic, even though the others tell me to just play.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I still love my family, but many days I just sit at the kitchen table, say very little, and let the garden and choir practice go by without feeling like moving.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: There are weeks when I wake up feeling so bright and important that I laugh loud and talk fast, but if someone I think they’re telling me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear a voice speaking when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… in the afternoon mostly. House gets real quiet, and I hear my old friend call my name from out by the porch. Once in a while it’s like he’s humming one of them choir songs. Sounds clear… like he’s right there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear that voice, does it ever tell you to do things?\n\nPatient: No… not telling me to do things. Mostly it just calls my name, or hums a church song… like he’s out there on the porch.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences started, did you have a stroke, head injury, seizure, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not that I can remember. Nothing like that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Maybe… about nine months, I’d say. Not every day. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. I still love my family, I do. But a lot of days I just sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden… choir practice… I just let it pass. Don’t feel much like getting up for it.\n\nDoctor: Did these experiences start suddenly, over just a few days?\n\nPatient: No… not over just a few days. It’s been more… gradual, I guess. Since after my close friend passed away. About a year and five months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not most of the day, I don’t think. I get low sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m sure about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your emotions feel blunted or that it’s hard to show your feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe. I still care about my family, but I don’t show much, I guess. Most days I just sit there at the kitchen table and don’t say a whole lot. The garden, choir… I just let it go by. Don’t feel like moving much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with your memory?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… maybe little things now and then, but I haven’t really noticed anything big with my memory.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor told you that you have Parkinson’s disease?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anyone’s told me that.\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medicines in the months before these experiences began?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I can’t think of any new pills around then. Nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if they’re trying to hurt me exactly… but the men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio man, they say things that feel meant for me. Like warnings. My wife says it’s just talk, but… I know I need to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Has any doctor ever told you that you have dementia or Alzheimer’s disease?\n\nPatient: No… not that I remember. Nobody’s told me Alzheimer’s or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if someone can put thoughts into your mind or take thoughts out of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about putting thoughts in or taking them out… it’s more like messages. The men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they say things that feel meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, but… I know what I hear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any brain scan or medical test that showed a problem with your brain?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I might’ve had tests before, but nobody told me there was something wrong with my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to get yourself to do everyday things, even simple ones?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some days. I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and not say much. I care about my family, I do, but things like the garden or choir practice… I just don’t get myself moving to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you been told that you have any medical illness affecting your brain?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anybody’s told me that. Not that I remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of confusion?\n\nPatient: Confusion? I don’t know… not really that I’ve noticed. Sometimes I’m slow to answer, but I wouldn’t call it confusion.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that your speech has become hard to follow or jumbled?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anyone’s said that. Maybe I’m quiet sometimes, but jumbled? I haven’t been told that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
